# Xyst verification history

## Fresh Xyst live-data sources · 2026-10-27

- The active Xyst full profile (`network.enabled=true`) used local LM Studio `qwen/qwen3.5-9b` to fetch the GitHub repository REST API: HTTP 200, untruncated, retrieved at `2026-10-27T19:46:43.140Z`; Xyst persisted `task_complete verified=true`. The tool journal reports `default_branch=main` and `pushed_at=2026-10-27T19:39:51Z`. Proof.
- A separate session fetched New York current conditions from Open-Meteo at the exact source URL, HTTP 200 and untruncated at `2026-10-27T19:51:37.639Z`; `task_complete` verified the fetch. The returned observation was timestamped 15:45 America/New_York on a 900-second source interval, with 62 °F, feels-like 56 °F, 30% humidity, 0 mm precipitation, 4.5 mph wind, and WMO code 0. This is the latest sample provided by that API, not a continuous stream. Proof.
- With `models.onlineDiscovery=true`, Xyst's `hf search` used the live Hugging Face Hub API and returned 20 results for `Qwen/Qwen3.5-9B` at `2026-10-27T19:57:28.889Z`. The response included public model metadata and changing download/like counts. Proof.
- Xyst supports on-demand public HTTP/JSON retrieval, scheduled mission polling, and source-supported webhook or MCP resource notifications when configured. Private sources still require credentials or an MCP integration; general Brave-backed search still requires a stored API key.

## Live GitHub write replay after a real process crash · 2026-10-27

- Using the private acceptance repository's closed issue #1, a child mission posted a unique comment through the real GitHub API and terminated with exit code 73 immediately after GitHub returned success, before Xyst stored the output. The database retained a `running` external action. After the runner lease expired, a fresh runtime recovered it as `uncertain` and moved the task to `reconciliation_required`.
- The live API showed the comment count increase from 2 to 3. After explicit `completed` reconciliation, mission resume replayed the reconciled result and completed; the count remained 3 and the action row remained singular. A separate full-runtime restart replay test also kept the remote count unchanged. Crash-window evidence · Completed-action replay evidence.

## Latest continuation · completion evidence freshness correction · 2026-10-27

After broadening freshness invalidation to all shipped local writers and the desktop automation action, rebuilt the final current-source Linux AppImage: 335,662,892-byte artifact, SHA-512 Base64 `k1HiOIfuCHAJIvsGbHpKBIY62YnaKz4zpAEmfWenHKJ5XNzm192jqqufYTX/on3FyHGGlGrJhzpM7OmT0IcEWA==`; `latest-linux.yml` matches. The exact package started with healthy SQLite and full access, passed a WSLg renderer smoke (25 buttons, 1280×913), and completed a live `web_fetch` for the GitHub repository API with HTTP 200, `retrieved_at=2026-10-27T16:06:48.025Z`, and persisted `task_complete verified=true` in session `b2faf956-2a0e-44b9-ae25-aaf1b7e8aea5`. The full Linux source suite and AppImage build passed; see acceptance proof, tool calls, and UI screenshot. Model-backed coding and stale-evidence acceptance was completed on the immediately preceding AppImage here; the final source additionally invalidates evidence after office edits/creation, browser downloads, repository clones, preview output, and desktop actions.

A model-backed coding attempt using the Linux AppImage and Qwen3 4B found that `task_complete` could accept a successful test and file read even after a later `file_patch` changed the file. The later patch introduced a syntax error, so the accepted result was a false positive. This attempt is recorded as failed acceptance, not as a completed Linux coding task; its isolated fixture and saved calls are under `local acceptance evidence (kept outside the repository)`.

The runtime now checks persisted SQLite row order before accepting `file_read`, `run_process`, or `run_shell` evidence. A read becomes stale after a later write to its path or a later process execution; a process result becomes stale after later file mutations or another process execution. Unrelated evidence such as a directory listing remains valid. The regression test recreates the stale-read-after-patch case and accepts completion only after fresh readback.

Verification on the Windows source: `npm test --workspace @xyst/runtime` passed 158 tests, with 2 host-specific skips and no failures; `npm run typecheck --workspace @xyst/runtime` passed. The Linux source passed all workspace typechecks, 157 runtime tests with 3 host-specific skips, 19 CLI tests, and 5 release checks. `git diff --check` passed with existing LF/CRLF normalization warnings.

The corrected source was rebuilt as Linux x64 AppImage local acceptance evidence (kept outside the repository) 335,658,866 bytes. Its `latest-linux.yml` matches SHA-512 Base64 `gzNUICXZKFWtp1aZ7olnys88GuVlh9bF6CHsf4FpmRf1GQQpUOixB19eYlgn9ktuhSOVPqlddX70YsTLZNBVhQ==`. The exact package passed isolated CLI startup with a healthy database and full access profile. Using Qwen3 4B, its model-driven session resumed the failing test, applied the zero-delay correction, reran all four tests successfully, read the final source, and completed with persisted `task_complete verified=true` (session `9a4ae2ed-4ff6-4ad8-ba3e-b059c9f9bb73`). The WSL LM Studio server was loopback-only; both Qwen3 4B instances started for these checks were unloaded afterward, leaving the preloaded Qwen3 0.6B intact. See proof, persisted tool calls, and the final source fixture.

## Packaged live API mission recovery after runner crash · 2026-10-27

The exact current-source Windows package ran mission `live-mission-crash-recovery-20261027` with a local `mistralai/ministral-3-3b` model. The acceptance harness killed the Xyst CLI runner after task `write-marker` had persisted as completed and before dependent task `fetch-report` began. After the runner lease expired, `mission resume` recovered the same run: task 1 remained completed with one attempt; task 2 fetched the exact public GitHub API URL with HTTP 200 at `2026-10-27T14:20:06.464Z`, created and read back `live-report.md` with `default_branch=main` and `pushed_at=2026-10-27T08:53:41Z`, and completed with persisted `task_complete verified=true`. The first continuation stopped after the file read because 8,192 context tokens were insufficient; resuming the saved task at a verified 12,288-token model context completed the mission. Final state, crash-injection record, task-session calls, and workspace are preserved under the acceptance folder.

One acceptance detail exposed a verifier limitation: task 1 requested the exact line `recovery marker: once`, while the model created and read back only `once`; the generic evidence checker still marked the natural-language content criterion verified. Therefore this run proves checkpoint preservation and successful live API-to-file recovery, but it does not prove exact literal content criteria are enforced. The structured assertions and current automatic literal inference now reject that mismatch; this historical run is not counted as exact-content proof. This is a distinct run from the earlier failed mission above.

## Live API acceptance · 2026-10-27

After confirming the current local runtime was healthy, `network.enabled=true`, and the full access profile was active, Xyst loaded the installed `qwen/qwen3.5-9b` model at 16,384 context. Its saved agent session `05bc837e-7017-4814-bc56-c83fbc91b358` called Xyst's own policy-checked `web_fetch` against two live JSON APIs. GitHub returned HTTP 200 at `2026-10-27T13:02:06.113Z`, with `default_branch=main` and `pushed_at=2026-10-27T08:53:41Z`. Open-Meteo returned HTTP 200 at `2026-10-27T13:02:06.594Z`; its New York current observation was 09:00 EDT, 54.2 °F, feels like 47.9 °F, 54% humidity, 8.3 mph wind, 0 mm precipitation, WMO code 0. After resume, Xyst's persisted `task_complete` call verified both requested source URLs. The model remains loaded for reuse.

The available live-data routes are public HTTP(S) JSON/REST via `web_fetch`, Brave Search when its key is stored, dedicated GitHub and Hugging Face clients, and configured authenticated MCP integrations. This validates on-demand retrieval, not an always-on stream; scheduled polling and source-supported webhooks or MCP subscriptions provide updates over time. The active account has GitHub CLI authentication; no Brave Search key or Hugging Face token was configured during this check. Hugging Face online model discovery was off at first; it has now been enabled globally and `xyst hf search "Qwen3.5"` returned live model records from Hugging Face without requiring a token for public discovery.

## Linux live-model coding acceptance through the current AppImage runtime (2026-10-27)

- The Linux test repository was intentionally seeded with a broken retry-delay function and three tests. LM Studio's local WSL library initially had Qwen3-VL-2B and Qwen2.5-VL-3B. Those coding attempts did not pass: the 2B model repeated reads and wrote one invalid patch, while the 3B model narrated a proposed change without tool calls. These failed attempts remain in `local acceptance evidence (kept outside the repository)` and are not counted as acceptance.
- Downloaded `qwen/qwen3-4b-2507` from the LM Studio catalog (2.50 GB), loaded it at 12,288 context, and ran the Xyst CLI from the current Linux AppImage's unpacked payload with an isolated XDG profile and local LM Studio endpoint. The model used Xyst `file_patch` to author the capped exponential backoff and input-validation implementation.
- The editing session hit its turn limit and did not complete its own evidence check. A fresh packaged-runtime Xyst session, `9f026f92-15b9-420c-855d-e7cd540118c7`, verified the persisted result: `run_process` ran `npm test` with exit code 0 and all 3 tests passing; `file_read` read the changed source; `task_complete` returned `verified=true`. No test or product source file was changed by the verification session.
- The temporary WSL model instance and LM Studio server were stopped after verification. The 2.50 GB model remains downloaded in the WSL LM Studio library. Windows live-model coding acceptance is recorded above; this closes a matching small Linux package/runtime/model/tool/readback acceptance. Broader task/model quality remains open.

## Live LM Studio repository-change acceptance with Qwen 3.5 9B (2026-10-27)

- Loaded `qwen/qwen3.5-9b` through the local LM Studio server at `127.0.0.1:1234`, initially with an isolated identifier and 8,192 context. The initial Xyst agent attempt correctly saved rather than running when its prompt estimate was 8,201 tokens. Resumed session `3fc4fafc-41b0-4d8d-b3f1-542baa45b5e1` after loading the same model at 12,288 context.
- In disposable Git repository `local acceptance evidence (kept outside the repository)`, the model used `file_patch` to replace an intentionally wrong retry-delay implementation with capped exponential backoff and input validation. Xyst's `run_process` executed `npm test`; all 3 tests passed, compared with 2 failures in the initial baseline. Xyst read back the final source and accepted persisted process and file evidence with `task_complete verified=true`. Final source SHA-256: `b7bc4675a1d569adb4fee72bd08422d391fcf041af511e210d90db1787f9df3c`.
- The first process call failed before startup because Windows could not spawn the `npm` command shim. Xyst classified that as no-effect, retried with the executable form, and the test command succeeded. The model run used an isolated Xyst profile and acceptance workspace; the temporary model was unloaded afterward. The pre-existing Nemotron instance remained idle.
- This is a Windows live-model coding/tool-use acceptance on a small disposable repository. It does not yet establish broad product-repository coding quality or equivalent live-model acceptance on Linux.

## Live resource coordination and cold-model queueing (2026-10-27)

- The first isolated two-task Qwen 3.5 9B acceptance exposed an initial-wave cold-start race: both tasks were admitted as if one model instance could be shared before either task had loaded it. LM Studio created two 6.55 GB instances; NVIDIA memory reached 15,382 MiB used and 596 MiB free. Xyst cancellation stopped the isolated mission before any tool call or file write, and both test instances were unloaded.
- Changed `planResourceAwareBatch` to reserve at most one cold start per model per scheduling wave. Other tasks for the same cold model are deferred for re-planning after inventory confirms residency; separate cold models each retain their full memory/VRAM estimate. Resident-model workers keep the existing incremental estimate. Added regression coverage for same-model reservation, distinct-model GPU costs, and resident-model sharing.
- Re-ran the live two-task mission with Qwen 3.5 9B. The scheduler selected one cold start and deferred the second task. After task one completed, backfill admitted task two against the single resident instance. Both tasks created and read their exact marker, `task_complete` verified both, mission status was `completed`, peak active tasks was 1, and runtime inventory recorded one Qwen instance. Mission `272ae0d5-e5d4-4437-a8a7-bd637e98963d`, run `1d3ff39c-943b-43c8-b596-8df55acb645d`; model unloaded after acceptance.
- Windows runtime suite: 154 passed, 2 host-dependent skips, 0 failures. Runtime typecheck passed. Acceptance files, SQLite journals, and marker readbacks are preserved in `local acceptance evidence (kept outside the repository)`.

## Live-model mission recovery after process termination (2026-10-27)

- Used Qwen 3.5 9B at 14,592 context in an isolated Xyst data directory and workspace. A two-task mission created a marker file, then required a dependent readback task.

## Current-source Qwen 3.5 coding acceptance (2026-10-27)

A live LM Studio-backed Xyst agent completed a small isolated coding task with actual tool calls. Xyst created and patched `src/is-even.mjs` and `test/is-even.test.mjs` inside `local acceptance evidence (kept outside the repository)`; the implementation uses `Number.isSafeInteger` and parity, and the tests cover integer boundaries, invalid types, fractions, non-finite values, and unsafe integers. The final Node test run passed 15/15.

- Final verification session `f990d7aa-8d95-404a-a436-0391b5bb6f50` is persisted as `completed`; `task_complete` accepted the exact source and test `file_read` calls and the successful `run_process` result. Earlier Xyst sessions in the same isolated workspace contain the actual create/patch calls and the initial failed test runs; model narration was not counted as evidence.
- Qwen 3.5 9B was loaded through Xyst at a 16,384-token context after LM Studio estimated 7.57 GiB GPU memory. That single instance was unloaded after verification. During the earlier 8,192-context retries, the growing saved conversation caused additional 9B instances to load at 11,008, 13,312, and 15,872 tokens with automatic unloading disabled. GPU use reached about 15.4 GiB; the server stopped responding on port 1235 and was reachable again on port 1234. The four temporary 9B instances were unloaded. Context-growth model reuse/unload behavior remains an open resource reliability issue.
- The acceptance files and isolated profiles are under `local acceptance evidence (kept outside the repository)`; repository source files were not changed by this task.

## Context-growth model load reserve (2026-10-27)

The same live task showed that the 2,048-token default reserve could be exhausted after a few tool calls, causing a resumed session to load additional context-sized instances of the same model. Increased the shared planned load reserve to 8,192 tokens; the LM Studio resource estimate and actual model load both use this value, capped at the declared model context. Runtime regressions cover initial loading and mission resource estimates. A fresh live task used one 14,592-token Qwen 3.5 9B instance through eight tool calls and a resumed process-evidence check without loading another instance; that temporary model was unloaded afterward.

Windows `npm test` passed with 153 runtime passes, 2 host-dependent skips, 19 CLI passes, and 3 release checks. Ubuntu 24.04 WSL with Node 22.12.0 passed with 152 runtime passes, 3 host-dependent skips, 19 CLI passes, and 3 release checks. Runtime, CLI, and desktop typechecks passed on both platforms.

## Natural-language command completion evidence (2026-10-27)

A live Xyst request phrased “run a Node command” completed from a file read alone, despite having also run a process. `inferExplicitToolEvidence` now maps natural-language requests to run/execute/launch/start a command, script, program, executable, or tests to required `run_process` evidence. A deterministic regression rejects completion when only `file_list` supports the task and accepts it after a successful process result.

Live session `875cc4df-477b-43ee-9961-ea251fd3763f` completed with two distinct evidence records: the exact `file_read` for `proof.txt` and a successful `run_process` result with exit code 0 and stdout `COMMAND_OK`. Its `task_complete` journal verifies both tool types. The task used the same loaded 14,592-token Qwen instance as the context-reserve check and did not create another model instance.

## Process-start recovery regression in integrated source (2026-10-27)

- A persisted live Xyst coding session requested `run_process` with executable `npm`. On Windows, `spawn npm ENOENT` proves no child process started, but the runtime classified it as uncertain and halted the agent. Inspected the call's saved arguments and error; the requested npm script did not run. Reconciled the exact call as retryable.
- Added `ProcessStartError` for synchronous `spawn` exceptions and asynchronous child errors before the `spawn` event. `run_process` now records those as definite failures so the agent can correct the request. Errors after process start, including a timeout after writing a marker file, remain uncertain and require reconciliation.
- Added agent regressions covering a missing executable, a synchronously invalid NUL argument, recovery to a successful process that writes a marker, and a started process that writes a partial effect before timing out. Windows full verification: runtime 149 passed with 2 host-dependent skips, CLI 19 passed, release checks 3 passed; all workspace typechecks passed. The same patch on PR #1 passed hosted Windows/Ubuntu CI and package workflows (8 jobs).
- A second live trace showed `task_complete` could arrive in the same assistant response as file writes and reads, while its evidence indexes still referred to the pre-response snapshot. The generic out-of-range error did not explain that those just-created calls became citeable in a fresh turn. The runtime now identifies newly persisted evidence, includes the refreshed index list in its error, and instructs the model to verify on the next turn. A focused regression checks the stale error, exact refreshed labels, and subsequent successful completion. After this addition, full Windows results were runtime 150 passed with 2 host-dependent skips, CLI 19 passed, release checks 3 passed; workspace typechecks passed.
- A live-model retry reached a real Node test process after recovery, but the model-generated `.cjs` test used ESM/Jest syntax and failed. Subsequent model loading/inference returned HTTP 500; no verified task completion was recorded. Isolated files, profile, and session are preserved under `local acceptance evidence (kept outside the repository)`.
## Automatic Hugging Face acquisition and tool-using agent acceptance (2026-10-26)

A clean Xyst profile in an isolated Ubuntu 24.04.5 KVM guest began with an empty LM Studio LLM library. Automatic agent acquisition queried Hugging Face, downloaded a pinned GGUF, recovered delayed LM Studio inventory visibility, and then performed a real capability probe before allowing the model to handle task tools.

- The first bounded tool-use candidate, `ewin-reg/MiniCPM5-1B-Agentic-Tooluse-v3-GGUF`, did not emit the required exact `probe_tool` call. Its model-card claim was not treated as proof. Xyst excluded that exact pinned revision and continued to the next bounded candidate.
- Xyst then acquired `NovachronoAI/LFM2.5-1.2B-Nova-Function-Calling-GGUF`, Apache-2.0, at Hugging Face revision `a0b095b4c6c8470f40f87fcf3e538fb0ba1002c8`. The file was 730,894,912 bytes with SHA-256 `5d039ad4195447cf4b6dbee8f7fe11f985c01d671a18153084c869077e431fbf`. A live `probe_tool` call returned the exact expected arguments `{"status":"ready"}`; LM Studio inventory declared `trained_for_tool_use=true`.
- The agent reused that verified model in session `b7dbfe16-9550-4258-ae84-900742dbc315`. It called `file_create` on `/home/xystqa/Xyst-current-checkout/acquisition-proof.txt`, then called `file_read` on that exact path. The readback was exactly `XYST_AUTO_ACQUIRE_OK`, one line, SHA-256 `23d13cd1511fbe881878423762c503bb0f4cf83a54f3825df6e67bfe7817ea4c`. A resumed `task_complete` call cited the successful read evidence; persisted session status is `completed`.
- Cross-platform workspace validation after these changes: Windows `npm test` passed with 147 runtime tests, 2 host-dependent skips, 19 CLI tests, and 3 release checks. Linux `npm test` passed with 144 runtime tests, 5 host-dependent skips, 19 CLI tests, and 3 release checks; Linux `npm run typecheck` passed runtime, CLI, and desktop. `git diff --check` passed. Linux-only selection fixtures and child-process module paths were made portable; no selection behavior was weakened.

This closes source-runtime acceptance for automatic discovery, acquisition, integrity checking, LM Studio import/reconciliation, real tool-call capability verification, and subsequent agent file work. Exact current-source Windows and Linux package candidates are now built and smoke-checked in isolated acceptance directories; neither is published. See [model acquisition](./model-acquisition.md) and [build status](../BUILD_STATUS.md).

## Current-source live public API access (2026-10-26)

The current TypeScript runtime exercised its policy-checked `web_fetch` tool against two real APIs with the full access profile and `network.enabled=true`:

- Open-Meteo current conditions for New York City: HTTP 200, `application/json`, complete response, retrieved `2026-10-26T19:13:48.333Z`. The payload reported observation time `2026-10-26T15:00` in `America/New_York`, temperature 71.4 °F, relative humidity 31%, and WMO code 0.
- GitHub public repository API for `iExyst/Xyst-Ai-Agent`: HTTP 200, `application/json`, complete response, retrieved `2026-10-26T19:13:48.611Z`. The payload reported default branch `main` and `pushed_at=2026-10-26T19:01:40Z`.
- The actual Xyst CLI agent loop, connected to the installed LM Studio server at `http://127.0.0.1:1235`, used the already-loaded `qwen3-0.6b:2` model to fetch the same Open-Meteo endpoint. Its persisted tool result was HTTP 200 and untruncated, retrieved `2026-10-26T19:15:52.203Z`, with an observation at `15:15` EDT (71.7 °F, 30% humidity, WMO code 0). Session `3e963531-b456-4020-990c-e01c4f92dffe` completed after resume and verified the saved fetch evidence. The initial same-turn completion call preceded the refreshed evidence list and was rejected; resuming the persisted session allowed successful verification. The model was already loaded; no download or additional model load occurred.

This validates live public API reads through Xyst's current shared runtime. Public HTTP(S) JSON endpoints do not need provider credentials. Authenticated GitHub/Hugging Face routes use their configured integrations; private APIs need their provider credentials or an MCP server. Brave web search needs a Brave Search API token. For recurring updates, missions can poll on a schedule; signed GitHub webhooks and supported MCP resource subscriptions provide event-driven updates. A single `web_fetch` call is on-demand, not a continuous stream. See [general tools](./general-tools.md#web-retrieval), [active settings](./configuration.md#active-settings), and [credential storage](./configuration.md#credential-storage).

## OpenAI-compatible Responses API and current release packages (2026-10-25)

- Added `LMStudioProvider.responses()` and `streamResponses()` for `POST /v1/responses`, plus `xyst lm responses <request.json> [--stream]`. Request files are bounded to 1 MB and allow-listed; credentialed remote MCP tools resolve header references from the OS vault and require network, integration, and external-action capability while refusing Xyst per-tool approval mode. Response payloads and HTTP error bodies are redacted. Streaming handles LF/CRLF SSE, cancellation/deadlines, errors, and requires a terminal `response.completed` or `response.incomplete` event. Function calls remain response data; the Xyst agent loop continues to execute local tools through its journal.
- Windows typecheck and current full tests pass: runtime 126 passed / 2 host-dependent skips; CLI 13/13. Ubuntu 24.04 WSL full tests passed at 125/128 runtime (3 host-specific skips) and CLI 13/13; after the final SSE terminal-boundary change, the two Responses runtime tests passed on both Windows and Ubuntu. The Windows Responses tests also verify request/state fields, bearer auth, redaction, CRLF streaming, and truncated-stream rejection. No actual model was loaded or queried.
- Current Windows NSIS: 267,510,777 bytes, SHA-512 `DVUq0IWpwKNjKxTikqomlGQIZkMLSMavlHm/JJV0SUhaWy5F51fNgQRla7l3q0E4J433c2EjgWMYHKV82eAZog==`, embedded `app.asar` SHA-256 `7DC6330E75A8C5BDD69A666F7BBB9CA642D11A1D479539EE2F6E01207468DB65`. ## NVIDIA-aware model and mission resource selection checkpoint (2026-10-24)

It prefers a sampled candidate whose estimated GPU memory fits current free VRAM and records estimate/free-memory rationale. Mission admission samples up to eight models, uses the same live free-VRAM reading to coordinate parallel work, accounts for resident-model additional GPU usage with a disclosed 12% heuristic, and serializes unknown or over-budget estimates to one fallback task. ## Ready-task resource backfill checkpoint (2026-10-24)

- Mission runners now dispatch another already-ready task when an admitted task completes and frees a concurrency slot. Each backfill rechecks current free RAM and refreshes NVIDIA VRAM telemetry when available; it defers an over-budget fallback while another task is active. Distinct-workspace, retry, cancellation, pause, and uncertain external-action handling remain on the same task execution path. After each completion the runner refreshes mission-task state and backfills newly unlocked dependency tasks when their workspace and resource estimates fit. It rebuilds the bounded prompt and LM Studio GPU estimate when dependency results increase required context.
- Windows runtime typecheck passes; 108 runtime tests pass and two environment-dependent tests skip. Ubuntu 24.04 WSL / Node 22.12 workspace typecheck and all 110 runtime tests pass. A regression test holds one task open and confirms queued work starts in the released slot before the held task ends.
- Fresh Windows NSIS and Ubuntu x64 AppImage packages include the backfill implementation. Their updater manifests match artifact byte size and SHA-512; the unpacked Windows and Linux runtimes contain the dynamic resource-plan path.

## Dependency-aware resource backfill checkpoint (2026-10-24)

- Newly unlocked dependency tasks can now use a freed slot while other tasks in the same batch remain active. The runner refreshes persisted DAG state, serializes workspace claims, reconstructs each dependent prompt from completed result JSON, and resamples LM Studio GPU estimates when the maximum context estimate grows. Explicitly unknown future results are never estimated in advance.
- Windows runtime typecheck passes; 109 runtime tests pass, two environment-dependent tests skip, and all 10 CLI tests pass. Ubuntu 24.04 WSL / Node 22.12 workspace typecheck passes; all 111 runtime and all 10 CLI tests pass. A regression holds an independent task open, verifies its dependent starts in the freed slot, and confirms the GPU estimator receives the dependency-expanded context.
- Fresh Windows NSIS and Ubuntu x64 AppImage packages include dependency-aware backfill. Both updater manifests match artifact byte size and SHA-512; both unpacked runtimes contain the refreshed DAG, dependent-context, and GPU re-estimation paths.

## Latest ASAR packaging verification (2026-10-24)

Electron ASAR is enabled. The packaged CLI, runtime files, native dependencies, and bundled Node executable are available on disk under `app.asar.unpacked`, so the desktop can launch the CLI without storing the whole app tree as loose files. Packaged startup creates an `Xyst` workspace under Electron's OS-reported Documents directory. Windows `npm run typecheck` and the Windows/Linux package builds passed. The current Windows installer was installed into an isolated workspace directory, its bundled CLI/SQLite path ran, and the uninstaller removed that installation. Both packaged CLIs started through their respective bundled Node runtimes and read `documents.officeRendererExecutable`; the Linux app opened under WSLg, initialized SQLite, and created its default workspace. This root-only smoke needed `--no-sandbox` and does not verify native Linux desktop behavior. Windows NSIS: `release-asar-win/Xyst Setup 0.1.0.exe` (267,346,391 bytes; SHA-256 9C4B079CAE2168AF046D34E0691A7D62A8B85A388A6D4D7370F357E34C24D20B; `app.asar` 2043960 bytes). Ubuntu x64 AppImage: `release-asar-linux/Xyst-0.1.0.AppImage` (323,062,824 bytes; SHA-256 2DE9FE5FE9E4E8D289D9511970CAA8DB3151F96C512AA7E6C2CD937F5BA608A4). Both update manifests match the package size and SHA-512. The previous large loose-file artifacts remain in their separate release directories.


## Previous verified checkpoint (2026-10-24)

Scanned PDF OCR now supports bundled offline English (eng), French (fra), German (deu), and Spanish (spa) data. The documents.ocrLanguages setting defaults to ["eng"], validates unique supported codes, resolves through scoped runtime configuration, and is editable in CLI config and desktop Settings. Office PDF reads and CLI attachment extraction use the effective language list. OCR regression tests recognize French accented text with fra and English plus French with eng+fra; cross-platform typecheck and suites also pass (Ubuntu Node 22.12: 100 runtime and 10 CLI; Windows: 99 runtime, 1 symlink privilege skip, and 10 CLI). The additional data packages add about 25 MiB unpacked to the dependency tree. Fresh Windows NSIS and Ubuntu x64 AppImage artifacts were rebuilt under release-multilingual-ocr-win/ and release-multilingual-ocr-linux/. Both generated update manifests match exact artifact sizes and SHA-512 hashes. Packaged CLI setting round-trips and packaged French accented-text OCR passed on both platforms; the Windows package is 267,152,773 bytes (SHA-256 3B72F089EBC4FF5F6648C32AF078B7A500337D9F70472FF989C78AE8BF1399FE), and the Linux AppImage is 322,779,839 bytes (SHA-256 A0CCBB7AAB5C6184BB9C523E47711985F504517A5A69CBE5AB9927F3F7706D78).

The evidence-based task completion flow now accepts one successful tool result for multiple listed criteria when the model asserts that result supports each criterion. Regression coverage exercises the single-result case. Windows typecheck passes; Windows tests pass (99 runtime, 1 symlink privilege skip; 10 CLI). Ubuntu 24.04 WSL under Node 22.12 passes typecheck and all 100 runtime plus 10 CLI tests. A live WSL mission using `qwen/qwen3-vl-2b` created `/tmp/xyst-mission-e2e-20261024-07/workspace/hello.txt`, read back exact content `XYST_MISSION_OK`, and completed after persisted evidence checks for both mission criteria. The smaller `qwen/qwen3-0.6b` failed the same tool-use task, so this live result verifies the stronger installed model only.

The implementation follows the ordered build sequence supplied in the project brief. A milestone is marked complete only when its stated completion condition has been verified on both Windows and Linux where required.

Desktop agent execution now has a Stop task control that sends cancellation through the CLI IPC channel; inference, web and GitHub API requests, browser and MCP calls, Git subprocesses, and `run_process` receive the shared AbortSignal. ## Latest headless run notification support (2026-10-24)

- `notifications.enabled` now covers headless CLI agent and mission runs in addition to the foreground desktop. The CLI subscribes to shared runtime completion/failure events, resolves mission- and project-scoped overrides when available, and sends only a generic status message. Electron child CLIs set `XYST_DESKTOP_PARENT` to avoid duplicate desktop alerts. Settings or notifier failures are caught and cannot interrupt mission/runtime event handling. Native delivery uses `node-notifier` 10.0.1 because the Linux bundle currently ships Node 22.12; its Windows toast and Linux `notify-send` paths both returned successful callbacks in the current user and WSLg sessions. Automated event tests verify opt-in behavior, no task-content disclosure, event filtering, unsubscribe, and non-interference on settings/logging errors. Services require an interactive user notification session; Linux needs a D-Bus desktop notification service and Windows tasks need the logged-on user's notification session.
- Windows and Ubuntu 24.04 WSL pass full typecheck and 90 runtime plus 10 CLI tests. Fresh Windows NSIS (`release-notifications-final-win/Xyst Setup 0.1.0.exe`) is 232,176,025 bytes (SHA-256 `539403FD6AE4C6429E9FC19A95851FCEAA85CD72F26C1E8A3793A510A46D1415`); Ubuntu x64 AppImage (`release-notifications-final-linux/Xyst-0.1.0.AppImage`) is 246,056,237 bytes (SHA-256 `1FB08DBA4E402827E24BDA8BDCFFBBE5B9968EDA7757FD72B8C304D66DC13DCC`). Each update manifest's SHA-512 and size match its artifact. Packaged CLIs start with bundled Node and return the notifications setting; the Linux packaged notifier callback also succeeded in WSLg. Linux package and manifest are copied into the workspace.

## Latest structured office reading improvements (2026-10-24)

- `office_read` now parses CSV fields with quoted commas, escaped quotes, multiline records, BOM removal, UTF-8 validation, size/output bounds, and spreadsheet-style cell addresses. XLSX formula cells retain the expression and expose any cached value stored in the workbook separately; cached values may be stale, and Xyst still does not calculate formulas. Windows and Ubuntu 24.04 WSL both pass full typecheck and all 89 runtime plus 10 CLI tests; office-reader tests cover formula caches, quoted CSV fields, embedded newlines, and malformed quotes. Fresh Windows NSIS and Ubuntu x64 AppImage packages include these changes; both packaged CLIs start with bundled Node, manifests match artifact size and SHA-512, and unpacked runtime bundles contain the CSV and formula-cache behavior. Windows installer `release-office-win/Xyst Setup 0.1.0.exe` is 230,587,705 bytes (SHA-256 `EA86BE8FFD4ACB889BCD71F987844528D8B03B59C44BCC281348A2A41F3A2565`); Ubuntu AppImage `release-office-linux/Xyst-0.1.0.AppImage` is 245,621,666 bytes (SHA-256 `7CA7BE8E688D3C7109ACEFAE56680C27864D96CCC051911CD9AFB12D06A9B2FF`). The Linux artifact and update manifest are copied into the workspace. The packaged Linux desktop launches under this machine's WSLg session and opens its `Xyst Control Room` window, then runs its bundled CLI status queries. This verifies WSLg startup only; it does not replace native Linux-host or visual interaction checks. Visual office rendering, formula calculation, and image/OCR support remain unimplemented.

## Current scope

The runtime creates a versioned SQLite database for sessions, messages, tool calls, missions, agents, jobs, artifacts, usage, settings, model download records, and inspectable memory entries. Settings resolve in the order session → agent → mission → project → global, and return both value and source. Settings export/import omits values with secret-like keys. File patching checks the original SHA-256 and requires a unique match before atomically replacing content. Process execution uses argv by default, offers an explicit shell operation, streams output, supports timeouts, and propagates cancellation to the process tree. Desktop agent runs can be stopped through the CLI IPC channel; inference, web and GitHub API requests, browser and MCP calls, Git subprocesses, and `run_process` accept the run cancellation signal. Missions can be created with `mission create definition.json`, run from the CLI, and kept scheduled in `mission daemon`. Hugging Face search is opt-in with `model discovery on`; downloads also require `model downloads on`. CLI and desktop agent tasks accept bounded UTF-8 text, selectable-text PDF, and PNG/JPEG/WebP attachments; structured message content persists for resume and images require a model declaring vision support.

## Known gaps

- A live authenticated issue-to-PR run against the private validation repository created PR #2 and passed its readme-smoke check. Live GitHub Enterprise Server verification remains unavailable.
- A native Linux distribution desktop is not available in this workspace. Ubuntu 24.04 WSLg has verified the X11/Xwayland desktop automation adapter against a disposable window and launched the packaged app. From the packaged Linux CLI, the user systemd service installed, reached `active/running`, uninstalled, and returned to `not-found/inactive`; mission store was empty and no daemon remained. Native Linux Wayland/X11 desktop certification, clean-host library compatibility, and native-host service/lingering behavior still need verification.
- Mission tasks can run concurrently only when their ready workspace paths are distinct; shared paths remain serialized. Detached worktree creation/listing/cleanup and dependency workspace reuse are implemented. Named task-agent assignments, model/turn limits, expected outputs, completion criteria, restricted access profiles, lifecycle persistence, CLI inspection, task-level model/turn overrides, and per-task pause/resume/retry in CLI and desktop are implemented. The CLI and desktop now request local-model mission plans as bounded drafts; installed-model checks, DAG validation, and approximate memory-based concurrency limits are enforced. Drafts are not saved or run automatically. Resource-aware admission backfills released slots for already-ready and newly unlocked dependency tasks, refreshing DAG state, prompt context, and GPU estimates as required. Dynamic reassignment of explicitly pinned models remains unsupported, consistent with manual model override precedence. External side effects use a durable per-session or per-task argument hash; uncertain outcomes require explicit CLI reconciliation. A new cross-platform process-level test verifies that a SIGKILL after a real marker-file write recovers the same mission/session and reuses the completed action once. Crash injection during a GitHub API write remains unverified.
- Model download integration has both the LM Studio managed-download flow and a pinned Hugging Face streaming/import flow for automatic acquisition. When online discovery is enabled and no installed model declares adequate context for a non-image task, Xyst recommends only a public artifact with pinned metadata and a recognized context limit in its model config; automatic downloads remain separately opt-in. After integrity and capability checks, Xyst verifies the imported inventory context and requests the estimated task context on load. Unknown Hugging Face context metadata is not accepted for context-constrained acquisition. With discovery disabled, selection remains local-only and a known-short selected model is rejected before generation. Generic capability checks do not establish task-specific quality, tool accuracy, or long-context behavior.
- The LM Studio matrix was checked against official docs and the running Windows 0.4.26.0 install on 2026-10-24. It now records per-feature support and known limits; repeat this check before releases because the API and app may change. Top-k and repeat penalty are now scoped settings applied to agent requests and exposed in desktop; min-p and reasoning are available as native direct CLI request options, while custom-tool agent requests use LM Studio defaults for those endpoint-specific fields.
- Local model measurements distinguish inference responses from session task outcomes. Verified task outcomes use `task_complete` evidence and remain a proxy rather than an independent correctness evaluation; remote endpoints, cancelled runs, and intermediate models are excluded. Measurements stay in local SQLite and are excluded when `telemetry.localMetrics` is disabled. At least three task outcomes per candidate are required for task completion rates to affect automatic selection; inference metrics require at least three calls per candidate.
- The context estimate is a heuristic using text length and image count. Agent execution rechecks the estimate before each generation and preserves a saved session with an actionable error if conversation growth exceeds the selected model's reported context. Mission scheduling measures the bounded prompt it will send, including known GitHub trigger input and completed dependency results; future dependency outputs are not available until those tasks complete. NVIDIA VRAM is sampled for display and, when the default local LM Studio server/CLI is available, automatic single-agent selection compares up to three `lms load --estimate-only` GPU estimates with current free VRAM. Mission batch admission samples up to eight models and defers work exceeding observed free VRAM. Estimates do not guarantee placement; resident-model incremental KV-cache cost remains a 12% heuristic and no memory is reserved from other processes. Remote/non-NVIDIA GPU memory remains unavailable. The remaining platform-level verification requires a native Linux desktop session; the live GitHub issue-to-PR workflow has been verified against the private validation repository.
- Packaged apps bundle the same Node runtime used to install their native dependencies, so the CLI subprocess and SQLite module share an ABI. Opt-in update checks and explicit update installation are implemented for packaged Windows NSIS and Linux AppImage builds; live hosted-feed installation and publisher-authenticated signatures remain unverified. Windows Task Scheduler and Linux systemd user-service install/start/status/uninstall passed using the packaged CLIs (Linux under WSL); Linux daemon crash recovery also passes under WSL systemd. Native Linux host and lingering behavior, Windows reboot/restart recovery, and clean-host service installation still need verification.
- Desktop packages enable ASAR and unpack the CLI, runtime, native modules, and bundled Node under `app.asar.unpacked`, which lets the shared CLI subprocess load its files. The current native-automation packages were built and their update manifests verified; clean-host installer/runtime certification remains open.
- CLI and desktop tasks accept bounded UTF-8 text, selectable-text and scanned PDF (bounded offline OCR in configurable English, French, German, and Spanish), and PNG/JPEG/WebP attachments. Direct and full-agent live VLM inference are verified; the completion gate still reports tool-free visual answers as unverified. visual Office rendering now uses optional LibreOffice previews; layout differences from Microsoft Office remain; `office_preview` renders PDF and Office pages to offline PNG artifacts.
- Web search uses the Brave Search API and requires a user-configured API token; returned results are untrusted. Public HTTP(S) page retrieval extracts bounded text. Optional Playwright Chromium provides persistent per-session profiles, browser navigation and interaction, and workspace upload/download. The saved download size is bounded, but Playwright may use additional temporary space while transferring a file. Native Linux desktop/browser integration remains unverified; PDF pages can be previewed with `office_preview`, while DOCX, XLSX, and PPTX previews now use optional LibreOffice; Microsoft Office fidelity and complex-layout QA remain open. Office tools can read selectable PDF text and append a text overlay, but do not replace or redact existing PDF text; scanned pages now use bounded offline OCR in configurable English, French, German, and Spanish. DOCX body and PPTX slide text can be edited with a hash-checked replacement. Microsoft Office-equivalent layout fidelity remains open. MCP sampling is limited to bounded text prompts on the run's selected model; image/tool-assisted sampling and independent-server interoperability remain unverified. MCP OAuth login/status/logout is implemented; independently hosted OAuth interoperability remains unverified. MCP SDK v2 auto-negotiates protocol revision 2026-07-28 where available and falls back to legacy revision 2025-11-25; the local SDK HTTP integration test passes, while independently hosted modern-server interoperability remains unverified.
- The Linux AppImage was built and launched under WSLg on Ubuntu 24.04 after installing `libnss3` (which supplies `libnspr4`). Verify the target distribution's Electron runtime libraries on a clean desktop before public release.

- The CLI `agent chat` command provides a TTY-based interactive loop over the shared agent runtime; `--session` continues an existing session and `/exit` leaves the session saved. CLI background process jobs run in detached workers with persisted state, bounded/redacted output, process-tree cancellation, and queued interactive stdin exposed in CLI and desktop. CLI and desktop PTY jobs use node-pty on Windows and Linux, with xterm rendering, raw-key input, terminal control sequences, persisted output, resize requests, cancellation, and persisted job inspection. Native PTY tests pass on Windows 11 and Ubuntu 24.04 WSL. Desktop rendering has not yet been smoke-tested in a native Linux desktop session.

## Latest MCP SDK v2 and subscription verification (2026-10-24)

- MCP client/server production dependencies migrated to `@modelcontextprotocol/client` and `@modelcontextprotocol/server` 2.4.0. Version negotiation auto-detects modern revision 2026-07-28 and falls back to legacy 2025-11-25; SSE uses explicit legacy mode. The older 1.32.2 SDK is a devDependency for legacy interop fixtures only. MCP servers advertising resource subscriptions expose per-run subscribe, unsubscribe, and update-inspection tools with a 50-URI limit and last-50 URI-only notices. Legacy uses subscribe/unsubscribe requests; modern replaces the bounded URI filter stream and validates the server's honored filter. Per-server operations are serialized to keep concurrent edits to the active URI filter consistent. Run shutdown closes modern streams or concurrently unsubscribes legacy URIs. Desktop Settings edits validated server definitions using vault references. OAuth browser login/status/logout is available through the CLI and stores tokens in the OS credential vault; independently hosted modern-server/OAuth interoperability is not yet verified. Bounded text-only server-initiated sampling routes to the selected model.
- Final MCP test scope: Windows and Ubuntu 24.04 WSL pass full typecheck, 75 runtime tests and 10 CLI tests each. Regression coverage verifies automatic mission retries avoid the previous model when an eligible alternative exists; attempted model history persists in the task's durable agent definition, and exhausted inventories fall back to their best eligible model. Explicit assignments remain pinned. Legacy subscriptions use a v2 in-memory client/server wire test. Modern stream replacement, notification capture, unsubscribe, and cleanup have a fixture test; parallel subscribe/unsubscribe operations are serialized per server to preserve the complete active filter. The local HTTP test negotiates protocol revision 2026-07-28 against the SDK server handler, receives a `subscriptions/listen` resource update, rereads the changed resource, and closes the stream. This establishes SDK wire interoperability but does not replace testing against an independently hosted modern MCP server.
- Latest staged Windows NSIS installer (230,537,527 bytes, SHA-256 `F48CBC899BB16C41FB3E7980199386FCEE588D835135AEA9813661071D5FABEA`) and Ubuntu x64 AppImage (248,343,343 bytes, SHA-256 `D269D918D34D647442F69D3238C0CEFC3ADE3A6DD07CE79CC53156DD1621C23E`) include review-comment listing, inline comments, multi-line comments, and replies. Update manifests match artifact SHA-512 and sizes. Packaged CLIs start with bundled Node 24. Review comment and reply requests are documented against current GitHub REST APIs and fixture-tested on Windows and Ubuntu 24.04 WSL. The authenticated private validation repository and live PR/check result are recorded below.

## Latest GitHub webhook loop prevention (2026-10-24)

- GitHub webhook processing now compares `sender.login` with Xyst's authenticated actor before queuing mission triggers. Personal-token identity comes from `/user`; GitHub App identity comes from the JWT-authenticated `/app` response and is normalized to `slug[bot]`. The identity is cached for five minutes. Self-authored deliveries are marked processed and skipped; when a sender exists but Xyst cannot resolve its actor, handling errors instead of risking a self-trigger loop.
- Regression tests cover self-authored event suppression, durable processed state, external event queuing, and GitHub App actor identity resolution. Windows and Ubuntu 24.04 WSL pass full typecheck and all 82 runtime plus 10 CLI tests.
- Rebuilt Windows NSIS at `release-github-webhook-win/Xyst Setup 0.1.0.exe` (230,563,909 bytes, SHA-256 `5E22234491AD064EF5A33513F679D1DD6DB5979FAFD3A967840CA947AF8C054A`) and Ubuntu x64 AppImage at `release-github-webhook-linux/Xyst-0.1.0.AppImage` (248,359,759 bytes, SHA-256 `4EE806B66A14F95B61B8CA3CAA985AF05631053E03EAF0B65A7157B02C0EA7DA`). Both updater manifests match SHA-512 and size; bundled Node 24 CLIs start, and packaged runtime builds contain the webhook identity guard.

## Latest context-aware model selection verification (2026-10-24)

## Latest mid-execution user message support (2026-10-24)

- Desktop users can submit text guidance during an active agent run from the task and session forms. The desktop waits for a CLI queue acknowledgement before clearing the text. Messages are bounded to 64 KiB each and 20 pending per run; CLI rejects invalid, full-queue, or inactive-run messages.
- The shared runtime drains pending messages before the next model request and after a final answer before deciding the run is complete. This preserves assistant/tool-result ordering and saves received messages into the same session history. A final drain also preserves guidance arriving during the last tool cycle when the run reaches its turn limit or is cancelled; the queue closes synchronously after that drain so a late message is rejected instead of acknowledged and dropped.
- Windows passes full typecheck and all 86 runtime plus 10 CLI tests. Ubuntu 24.04 WSL passes full typecheck and all 86 runtime plus 10 CLI tests under Node 22.12.0. WSL's pre-existing `better-sqlite3` binary targeted Node 24, so the local addon was rebuilt for Node 22 before tests. Added regressions cover guidance arriving during inference and while a tool result is pending.
- Fresh Windows NSIS and Ubuntu x64 AppImage packages contain the message UI, IPC bridge, queue, and runtime changes. The packaged Windows CLI started with bundled Node 24 and passed an isolated `inference.reasoning` set/get round trip; Linux packaged CLI started with bundled Node 22.12.0 and read configuration successfully. Both renderer bundles contain the new message controls and both package runtimes contain the queue bridge. Update manifests match artifact hashes and sizes: Windows NSIS is 230,575,726 bytes (SHA-256 `C5DABA4667067CA423C3CFE624ED9D8B697061F56F61BC026EDD0A722E62322F`, SHA-512 base64 `ysgiK0EF3OCUpWV7v03vLNgZKP4qOgs8LkqJ2Go13U28XcPg1eCDKIBpszsDZyP+8EoEM/4PfHIlm93V7yz0aw==`); Ubuntu AppImage is 245,609,336 bytes (SHA-256 `54BC9651EACE196CE988EB55941AAF5C491897E5982A437237E5856F0C6040DA`, SHA-512 base64 `A0gvmphmNLUutWEVHm43fVBHbeUweAGAjxOCiytXaPnNl3Miqzd4/P/mQZPfuXHONaDdPLlk0ifLIsEU2XZfDw==`).

## Latest evidence-based task completion gate (2026-10-24)

- Agent sessions no longer infer completion merely because the model stopped calling tools. The model must call `task_complete` with every required criterion and successful saved tool-call evidence IDs. The runtime checks criteria coverage, evidence belongs to the active session/mission, evidence status succeeded, and rejects explicit error results or nonzero process exits. Any subsequent tool action or user guidance clears the verification. A final answer without successful verification is persisted as `unverified`; CLI returns exit code 3 and mission tasks fail as `COMPLETION_UNVERIFIED` without consuming automatic retries, remaining inspectable and explicitly retryable. This is an evidence gate, not an independent semantic proof that cited outputs satisfy the requested work.
- Regression coverage now includes missing-evidence rejection, a valid persisted filesystem evidence result, and mission failure without automatic retry. Windows and Ubuntu 24.04 WSL pass full typecheck and 89 runtime plus 10 CLI tests (WSL under Node 22.12.0).
- Fresh Windows NSIS at `release-evidence-win/Xyst Setup 0.1.0.exe` (230,584,866 bytes; SHA-256 `9E3F5C4A17A7DC456B126ADF55836B647B2180D4A43DE493329DA0631149EADA`) and Ubuntu x64 AppImage at `release-evidence-linux/Xyst-0.1.0.AppImage` (245,617,557 bytes; SHA-256 `A4FD0CF316BB860E503616473D8D0333486B872A3ADDC9E361EDB14AFF8528F1`) contain the gate. Their update manifests match exact sizes and SHA-512 digests. Packaged CLIs launched with bundled Node on both platforms and returned the reasoning-setting configuration; compiled runtime/renderer bundles include the verification tool and unverified status UI. Linux package and manifest were copied from the WSL build into the workspace release-evidence-linux directory for access.
## Latest restricted filesystem path hardening (2026-10-24)

- `AccessController.assertPath` resolves existing symlink targets and non-existing suffixes before restricted-root containment checks, then returns the caller path when its lexical location is within an allowed root or a canonical target when an in-root link points elsewhere inside the root. Agent file operations and office file tools consume the checked path; project instruction and local skill reads do the same. Full-access behavior remains unchanged. A regression covers allowed in-root symlinks and rejects read/write through links to out-of-root files.
- Windows passes full typecheck and all 90 runtime plus 10 CLI tests. The symlink regression is skipped because Windows symlink privilege is unavailable on this host. Ubuntu 24.04 WSL passes full typecheck and all 90 runtime plus 10 CLI tests under Node 22.12, including allowed and escaping symlink cases. Windows-only path assertions account for filesystem short-name normalization. Both platform packages were rebuilt from the hardened source and their manifests match artifact hashes and sizes (see the package verification checkpoint below).

## Persistent session compaction checkpoint (2026-10-24)

- Added versioned SQLite compaction snapshots. Compaction summarizes only a completed prefix with complete tool-call/result pairs, retains image-containing turns and the latest four raw messages, and rejects unsafe cut points. Original messages and the tool journal remain intact for `session show`, export, and recovery.
- Resume context uses the latest persisted summary plus raw messages after its boundary. The summary preserves exact user goals, task dependencies/states, outstanding and uncertain actions, and tool-call/result identity; agent instructions keep summaries as historical untrusted context. Snapshot deletion follows session deletion, and forks still copy the full transcript.
- Added `xyst session compact <id> [--model key]`, `xyst session summary <id>`, interactive `/compact [model-key]`, and a desktop Compact action. The selected model must be installed in LM Studio; compaction refuses remote endpoints to keep session data local.
- Added deterministic tests for persistence, unchanged transcript/journal, resume context, paired tool evidence, mission dependencies, uncertain actions, images, unpaired calls, and remote-endpoint refusal. Windows and WSL runtime/CLI suites and full typechecks pass; results are recorded in the verification update below.
- This is a product feature checkpoint and does not complete the combined Xyst build goal.

## Resource-aware Hub recommendation estimates (2026-10-25)

- `ModelAcquisitionService.recommend` now includes an assumed context (explicit requirement or up to 4096 tokens), declared context from a pinned config, artifact-size × 1.25 weight estimate, and an FP16 KV-cache estimate when layer/KV-head/head-dimension fields are available. Unknown KV dimensions are labeled weights-only. It reports free/total RAM and the effective budget (host bounds plus configured limit), saved max concurrency, aggregate separate-load memory, and full-offload memory against currently free VRAM on the largest single NVIDIA device. GPU and concurrency fit are estimates; the concurrent estimate does not assert shared-weight reuse.
- Recommendations surface up to six structured `cardData.eval_results` records (dataset, metric, reported value/date/source, and HTTPS source URL). Up to eight downloads-ordered search results are inspected; artifact choice ranks concurrent RAM fit, single-GPU fit, and quantization first. Across viable repositories, concurrent/GPU fit comes before Hub-listed evaluation evidence, with Hub downloads last. No evaluation values are compared or treated as independent quality evidence. Hub evaluations and model cards are publisher/community claims and require review. Official Hugging Face documentation describes structured model-card evaluation metadata and the newer `.eval_results` files: [model cards](https://huggingface.co/docs/hub/main/en/model-cards), [release checklist](https://huggingface.co/docs/hub/model-release-checklist).
- Desktop recommendation cards show the reported evaluation evidence; CLI recommendation objects include the same bounded evidence and explicit estimate assumptions. `docs/model-acquisition.md` documents formulas, uncertainty, shortlist bounds, and the fact that the single-agent budget filter and concurrent fit indicator are separate.
- Deterministic runtime fixture checks context, KV bytes, concurrency, disk, GPU estimate, configured-memory rejection, and evaluation-evidence ranking. Windows focused fixture and full runtime suite passed (117/119, two environment skips); CLI 10/10. Ubuntu 24.04 WSL focused fixture and full runtime suite passed (116/119, three Windows-only skips); CLI 10/10. Full workspace typechecks pass on both.
- Rebuilt packages: Windows NSIS `apps/desktop/release-model-estimates-win/Xyst Setup 0.1.0.exe`, 267,449,632 bytes, SHA-512 `b4NVZfg+44sjXQwfoRmOXorVNonQ1KOfYTT42PJdUqwyGYzOyZ+HpEGmW4LVzSzmzOox/8U0oTZyDU7mmsviMQ==`; Ubuntu x64 AppImage `apps/desktop/release-model-estimates-linux/Xyst-0.1.0.AppImage`, 323,013,590 bytes, SHA-512 `oShWphI7vn4Yvtto/USgNcjgUpMmZSZ/JduKg59Hwvean1+PEEh1dvrSdtKeVLyFmIjx8a2bINml/NuG2AXWcQ==`. Each updater manifest's SHA-512 and byte count matches its artifact. These package builds were not separately clean-installed; native Linux certification remains open.

# Local Linux AppImage updater smoke (2026-10-25)

- In a disposable Ubuntu 24.04 KVM guest, installed the current 0.1.0 AppImage into `/tmp` and served temporary 0.1.1 AppImage metadata and artifact from a loopback HTTPS feed.
- Packaged 0.1.0 discovered 0.1.1; explicit download completed at 323,021,746 bytes. `latest-linux.yml` declared that size and SHA-512 `n9ht0CF6gyFawMjFS6Sqb22kGLT5wFDaC5+ILmJ3zfZkZxCZzQV/nyoGyUHf5MGeI9LI+eq63Lc4ag1kqOPfQQ==`; both the downloaded file and the installed replacement matched.

- Electron rejected the guest-local test CA even after it was added to the guest trust store, so certificate errors were bypassed only in the disposable test process. This verifies the generic feed, download integrity check, and AppImage replacement path; it does not verify hosted certificate trust or publisher signing. The full download succeeded after electron-updater fell back from a failed differential attempt.

## Optional desktop sound alerts (2026-10-25)

- Added a local Appearance preference for a brief chime on mission or background-job transitions to a terminal success or failure state. The preference is stored locally and defaults to off; enabling it initializes Web Audio from the user's interaction, while reloading an enabled preference waits for the next user interaction to resume audio.
- Desktop TypeScript typecheck and production renderer build pass. In a disposable Ubuntu 24.04 KVM guest, the current AppImage's Control Room opened under Xvfb. CDP confirmed the sound preference was absent and unchecked on first launch, then persisted it after enabling. A real `/usr/bin/true` process job reached `completed`; an injected AudioContext observer recorded one oscillator start across repeated refreshes. A second completed process after disabling sound left the count unchanged. This verifies preference behavior and alert scheduling, not audible output on physical speakers.

## Exploratory Windows dashboard timing (2026-10-25; superseded by reproducible benchmark below)

- Measured the current packaged `desktop snapshot` through its bundled Node v24.20.0 CLI with isolated `APPDATA`/`XYST_ROOT` and `LM_STUDIO_BASE_URL=http://127.0.0.1:1` (no LM Studio server or model inference). Eight invocations exited 0: the first cold call took 886.2 ms; seven following calls took 499.0–517.1 ms, median 505.7 ms. The JSON snapshot was 6,239 characters.
- The first snapshot measured host RAM at 99,971,100,672 total / 28,951,379,968 used / 71,019,720,704 available bytes, and NVIDIA GeForce RTX 5080 VRAM at 17,094,934,528 total / 4,316,987,392 used / 12,437,159,936 free bytes. These are a single time-local resource sample, not Xyst-attributed usage.
- Dashboard refresh already consolidated 50+ separate CLI launches into one `desktop snapshot` call (recorded above). This baseline measures current latency; no prior wall-clock sample was collected, so it does not claim a measured wall-time speedup.

## Exploratory Linux dashboard timing (2026-10-25; superseded by reproducible benchmark below)

Its one resource sample reported WSL RAM at 48,978,657,280 total / 6,741,807,104 used / 42,236,850,176 available bytes and NVIDIA GeForce RTX 5080 VRAM at 17,094,934,528 total / 4,498,391,040 used / 12,255,756,288 free bytes. These are host readings at the sample time, not Xyst-attributed usage.
- Windows and WSL timings are separate platform baselines, not a controlled before/after comparison. No speedup is inferred from their difference.

## Reproducible packaged dashboard benchmark (2026-10-25)

- Added `scripts/benchmark-desktop-snapshot.mjs` and documented the method in [performance measurements](performance.md). The script accepts package Node and CLI paths, uses isolated temporary data/workspace directories, points LM Studio at an ephemeral local HTTP 503 stub, bounds output and runtime, and removes only its own temporary directory.

Cold: 507.0 ms; seven warm runs: 490.8–503.0 ms, median 497.4 ms; snapshot output 6,126 bytes. The resource snapshot reported 99,971,100,672 total / 29,032,312,832 used / 70,938,787,840 available host RAM bytes and 17,094,934,528 total / 4,504,682,496 used / 12,249,464,832 free NVIDIA VRAM bytes.
- Ran the same script against the Linux CLI/runtime entries whose hashes match the current AppImage, bundled Node v22.12.0, under Ubuntu 24.04 WSL. Cold: 528.4 ms; seven warm runs: 510.3–577.3 ms, median 543.8 ms; snapshot output 5,977 bytes. The WSL sample reported 48,978,657,280 total / 6,762,536,960 used / 42,216,120,320 available RAM bytes and 17,094,934,528 total / 4,500,488,192 used / 12,253,659,136 free NVIDIA VRAM bytes.
- Both runs used isolated state and the local rejecting server; all 16 snapshots exited successfully without querying a model. These measure snapshot CLI latency, not Electron painting or inference. ## Windows VM boot retry (2026-10-25)

## Mission schedule daylight-saving behavior (2026-10-25)

- Made cron behavior explicit in the mission guide: the IANA timezone is validated and used by Croner; a spring-forward nonexistent wall time fires at the equivalent time after the clock jump (New York 02:30 becomes 03:30 that date); a repeated fall-back wall time fires once at its first occurrence. Missed-run policy attempts at most one catch-up, and an overlapping trigger is rejected by the durable lease rather than queued.
- Added a deterministic regression check for the 2026 New York spring-forward and fall-back transitions. The complete runtime suite passed on Windows (124 passed, 2 host-dependent skips) and Ubuntu 24.04 WSL/Node 22.12.0 (123 passed, 3 Windows-only skips). No model was loaded or queried.

## Desktop scoped-settings editor and effective context resolution (2026-10-25)

- Added a Settings → Scoped configuration editor for all 52 documented settings. It selects a global, project, mission, agent, or session scope; loads the effective value/source; saves a JSON override; and clears an override to inherit. MCP setting arrays are validated by their existing schemas.
- Extended `config get` to resolve linked scope parents: missions include their project, agent IDs infer a unique parent mission or accept an explicit one, and mission-task sessions include the linked agent/mission/project. Multiple matching agent missions require explicit context rather than returning a misleading effective value.
- Added a CLI regression test that seeds all five levels and verifies session → agent → mission → project → global inheritance and `source`/`scopeId`. ## Clean Windows VM boot retry (2026-10-25)

- Investigated the clean Windows installation gate in Ubuntu 24.04 WSL with QEMU 8.2.2. Copied the Windows 11 ISO into WSL's Linux filesystem (9,047,330,816 bytes; local-copy SHA-256 `bd4307df32bc8af33b39ccecb1174aeb345386630f89a2b86c7a4e36b55ea650`) after the prior mounted-media attempt timed out. A fresh isolated QEMU VM with OVMF Secure Boot and a temporary TPM 2.0 reached the Windows media prompt, `Press any key to boot from CD or DVD`. After Enter, it returned to the UEFI Boot Manager; Windows Setup did not appear. No VM disk was attached, no Windows license screen was accepted, and no host installation or user data was changed. The temporary VM, TPM, and VNC processes were stopped. Clean Windows installation and GUI acceptance remain unverified.

## Context sizing, file-output evidence, and expanded live missions (2026-10-25)

- Before the source fix, automatic selection on an isolated profile chose `liquid/lfm2.5-1.2b` and loaded a 4,352-token context. LM Studio rejected the first prompt at 5,161 tokens. Increasing the estimate reserve from 4,096 to 6,144 tokens and loading with an additional 1,024-token cushion fixed the initial under-sized load; a later pre-fix run also showed why the runtime must check context after tool results grow the conversation. The current loader reads back the applied LM Studio context and refuses inference when it remains below the estimate.
- A separate current-source automatic-selection mission enabled local selection, loading, and switching while disabling remote fallback, Hugging Face discovery, and downloads. The runner selected `liquid/lfm2.5-1.2b`, then switched to `qwen3-4b-2507-instruct-uncensored-hauhaucs-aggressive` after the first local inference error. The switched model successfully read the input, created the exact declared output path, and reread the correct content, but returned `task_complete([3])` as plain text instead of a function call. Run `fd1e3cc8-5bba-46e0-b2cc-ea3cc12b32c0` ended `COMPLETION_UNVERIFIED`; this is not counted as a completed mission and live automatic tool-use quality remains open.
- The source now passes file-like mission `expectedOutputs` into the agent evidence checker. When a declared output path is a criterion, successful evidence must refer to that exact normalized path. A regression case creates a different file and confirms that it cannot satisfy the declared deliverable; the session remains unverified. This closes a concrete false-positive case found while testing model-generated paths.
- The exact hosted Windows package from PR head `f3e4cdd` also completed a larger live DAG before these source changes. With `qwen/qwen3.5-9b` at 16K context, two independent tasks created `alpha=7` and `beta=11` in distinct workspaces; the dependent task read both, wrote a third `summary.txt` containing `alpha=7`, `beta=11`, `sum=18`, reread it, and passed `task_complete`. Mission `live-qwen35-9b-parallel-dag-20261025`, run `558ce154-138b-4f92-ac1d-6e7c8f440a9d`; persisted task sessions were `a0b51ee7-e32b-4d0d-9aad-5ce54d7586c8`, `eb382ca6-465c-4e9e-9d2a-24e0e9a1fdf4`, and `685694a4-2021-455a-bc3e-cefa6fdf254c`. All file and task-completion calls succeeded.
- Latest local source checks after the context and path-evidence changes: `npm run typecheck` passed; `npm test` passed with 129 runtime tests, 2 host-dependent skips, and 14 CLI tests. The Windows LM Studio instances loaded for these checks were unloaded afterward; `lms ps` reported no models loaded. Linux's previously loaded Qwen3-VL instance and the registered Windows Xyst installation/profile were not changed.

## Hosted Windows package acceptance for context and output-path fixes (2026-10-25)

- PR code head `415ccc6` passed hosted Windows and Ubuntu CI ([run](https://github.com/iExyst/Xyst-Ai-Agent/actions/runs/40222164729)) and Windows/Linux package workflows ([run](https://github.com/iExyst/Xyst-Ai-Agent/actions/runs/40222164939)). Both Windows package jobs passed the clean-install and renderer smoke.
- Downloaded the exact `xyst-windows` artifact from package run `40222164939`. Its NSIS installer is 264,116,512 bytes with SHA-512 Base64 `nXZggRLN2i+NTf5L/NI2Vabjfg1JwYJwUF8WgEs8GR7+PkIJ6lu30B883e1si0kgv34wO2zZKZhihHmhYseEIg==`, matching `latest.yml`; embedded `app.asar` SHA-256 is `96DA3CD31E667E61AE9C674FB2C74F12146CE2A49EFE11ED91F99FDD1AAEA189`.
- First isolated attempt read the input, created `result.txt`, and reread it, but Qwen supplied evidence index `3`; the runtime correctly rejected that index because the available evidence list is newest-first and index `3` referred to the input read. That attempt was failed and is not counted as accepted.
- A fresh isolated profile and workspace then ran mission `hosted-windows-path-evidence-415ccc6`, ID `16a899b0-ac97-447a-9656-51ee761159d1`, run `232f0782-a11c-472b-ae71-55f82197542a`, with `qwen/qwen3.5-9b` at 16,384 context. Persisted session `c58572e7-b117-4fe8-950d-4468537b8889` records `file_read` of the input, `file_create` at the declared `result.txt`, `file_read` of that exact path/content, and successful `task_complete` with evidence index `[1]` bound to the output reread. On-disk content is `marker=hosted-415ccc6; sum=18`; both mission and task completed.
- The model was unloaded and `lms ps` reported no loaded models afterward. The registered Windows Xyst installation/profile was not accessed. All mission state and files were in `%TEMP%\xyst-live-415ccc6`.
- Follow-up source hardening makes the evidence list order explicit in both the `task_complete` tool description and per-turn system prompt: newest-first, with index 1 as the latest successful call. A Linux CI run exposed equal completion timestamps, so evidence ordering now breaks timestamp ties with SQLite rowid. Declared file-output criteria require a completed `file_read` result for the exact path; a file creation or patch alone cannot pass. Regression tests cover the prompt wording, wrong paths, ordering through the prose-nudge flow, and rejecting completion until reread. Local `npm run typecheck` and `npm test` passed (runtime 130 passed, 2 host-dependent skips; CLI 14 passed). The corrected exact Windows package from `806e443` passed package build and clean-install/renderer smoke; its automatic-selection mission is documented below.
- On the previous package from `c7265a7`, exploratory automatic-selection runs revealed two completion gaps: one run wrote `7+11=18` rather than the requested marker while only a path output was declared; another wrote the correct marker but attempted to complete from `file_patch` evidence without rereading. Neither run is counted as accepted. These results motivated adding a content criterion to the live check and requiring a successful readback for each declared file output.

## Adaptive automatic selection and malformed external-call recovery (2026-10-25)

- The isolated local LM Studio profile accumulated four unverified or failed task outcomes for `liquid/lfm2.5-1.2b`. Its inference responses succeeded, but it did not complete the required evidence checks. The previous selector waited for three inference samples and three task outcomes on both candidates before comparing task completion, so it repeatedly selected LFM.
- Updated selection to compare local verified task rates as soon as task outcomes exist and to prefer an eligible unmeasured candidate over one with measured zero completion. Mission resource admission now explores one RAM-fitting unmeasured model if every GPU-estimated candidate has zero verified outcomes; it records unknown GPU fit rather than treating the estimate as a fact.
- After rebuilding the shared runtime, the current-source CLI ran mission `current-source-auto-selection-measured-model` (`9e47fbb8-a5d9-4e24-be28-5d06df356b87`), run `b567bd74-4a43-4007-bcb9-c6138afc2afa`. Automatic selection chose `qwen3-4b-2507-instruct-uncensored-hauhaucs-aggressive`, loaded at 8,448 context, wrote `result.txt` in the declared isolated workspace, read back `XYST_AUTO_SELECTION_OK`, and passed runtime `task_complete` with file-read evidence. Mission state and output remain under `local acceptance evidence (kept outside the repository)`; the models loaded during these checks were unloaded afterward.
- A small-model attempt exposed an invalid JSON `run_process` call being labeled an uncertain side effect even though no durable intent row existed and no process ran. The agent now marks an external action uncertain only after its intent row has been persisted. A deterministic regression test covers this preflight parse case; the existing after-intent uncertainty behavior remains covered.
- Verification on the PR worktree: `npm test` passed (runtime 134 passed, 2 host-dependent skips; CLI 14 passed), `npm run typecheck` passed, and five focused selection/resource/recovery tests passed.

## Workspace-aware process replay with legacy journal compatibility (2026-10-27)

- `run_process` idempotency now includes the latest completed Xyst-managed file/office mutation row for the execution scope. It re-executes identical command arguments after Xyst edits the workspace and reuses a completed result when the workspace revision is unchanged. Legacy request hashes remain honored until a subsequent recorded mutation follows the legacy process row, preserving deduplication across app upgrades.
- Regression test seeds a pre-feature failed process result, verifies it is reused before edits, applies `file_patch`, verifies the identical process invocation executes successfully afterward, then resumes the task and confirms it does not append a duplicate process side effect.
